Fundamental Solutions and Gegenbauer Expansions of Helmholtz Operators in Riemannian Spaces of Constant Curvature This paper is a contribution to the Special Issue on Orthogonal Polynomials, Special Functions and Applications (OPSFA14). The full collection is available at https://www.emis.de/journals/SIGMA/OPSFA2017.html
Howard S. COHL †, Thinh H. DANG ‡ and T.M. DUNSTER §

Abstract
We perform global and local analysis of oscillatory and damped spherically symmetric fundamental solutions for Helmholtz operators ( − Δ ± β 2 ) \big({-}\Delta\pm\beta^{2}\big) in d d -dimensional, R R -radius hyperbolic 𝐇 R d {\mathbf{H}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}^{d} and hyperspherical 𝐒 R d {\mathbf{S}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}^{d} geometry, which represent Riemannian manifolds with positive constant and negative constant sectional curvature respectively. In particular, we compute closed-form expressions for fundamental solutions of ( − Δ ± β 2 ) \big({-}\Delta\pm\beta^{2}\big) on 𝐇 R d {\mathbf{H}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}^{d} , ( − Δ + β 2 ) \big({-}\Delta+\beta^{2}\big) on 𝐒 R d {\mathbf{S}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}^{d} , and present two candidate fundamental solutions for ( − Δ − β 2 ) \big({-}\Delta-\beta^{2}\big) on 𝐒 R d {\mathbf{S}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}^{d} . Flat-space limits, with their corresponding asymptotic representations, are used to restrict proportionality constants for these fundamental solutions. In order to accomplish this, we summarize and derive new large degree asymptotics for associated Legendre and Ferrers functions of the first and second kind. Furthermore, we prove that our fundamental solutions on the hyperboloid are unique due to their decay at infinity. To derive Gegenbauer polynomial expansions of our fundamental solutions for Helmholtz operators on hyperspheres and hyperboloids, we derive a collection of infinite series addition theorems for Ferrers and associated Legendre functions which are generalizations and extensions of the addition theorem for Gegenbauer polynomials. Using these addition theorems, in geodesic polar coordinates for dimensions greater than or equal to three, we compute Gegenbauer polynomial expansions for these fundamental solutions, and azimuthal Fourier expansions in two-dimensions.



keywords
hyperbolic geometry; hyperspherical geometry; fundamental solution; Helmholtz equation; Gegenbauer series; separation of variables; addition theorems; associated Legendre functions; Ferrers functions

1
Introduction

In this paper we derive associated Legendre and Ferrers function expressions for fundamental solutions of Helmholtz operators
\big({-}\Delta\pm\beta^{2}\big)
,
\beta^{2}>0
, in Riemannian spaces of constant curvature, namely in the
d
-dimensional
R
-radius hyperboloid
{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}^{d}
and hyperspherical
{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}^{d}
models with negative and positive sectional curvatures respectively, where
R>0
. We also compute eigenfunction expansions for these fundamental solutions of Helmholtz operators in geodesic polar coordinates in these Riemannian manifolds. In particular, we derive Gegenbauer polynomial expansions for spherically symmetric fundamental solutions of Helmholtz operators on Riemannian manifolds of negative-constant and positive-constant sectional curvatures. Useful background material relevant for this paper can be found in
[
19
,
28
,
32
,
35
]
.

This paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
, we introduce and give some useful properties for the special functions and orthogonal polynomials that we will use in this paper. In particular, we summarize (and derive new) large degree asymptotics for associated Legendre and Ferrers functions of the first and second kind. We also derive a collection of infinite series addition theorems for Ferrers and associated Legendre functions (generalizations and extensions of the addition theorem for Gegenbauer polynomials) which will be used to compute Gegenbauer polynomial expansions for fundamental solutions of Helmholtz operator in spaces of constant curvature. In Section
3
, for the hyperboloid model of
d
-dimensional hyperbolic geometry and for hyperspherical geometry, we describe some of their global properties, such as their respective geodesic distance functions, geodesic polar coordinates, Helmholtz operators, and their corresponding radial harmonics. In Section
4
, we show how to compute radial harmonics in a geodesic polar coordinate system and derive fundamental solutions for
\big({-}\Delta\pm\beta^{2}\big)
on
{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}^{d}
,
\big({-}\Delta+\beta^{2}\big)
on
{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}^{d}
, and study two candidate fundamental solutions for
\big({-}\Delta-\beta^{2}\big)
on
{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}^{d}
. In Section
5
, for
d\geq 3
, we compute Gegenbauer polynomial expansions in geodesic polar coordinates for fundamental solutions of Helmholtz operators on the hyperboloid and hypersphere. We also compute azimuthal Fourier expansions for these fundamental solutions in two-dimensions. In Appendix
A
, the proof of an addition theorem is presented.

2
Special functions, asymptotics, and notation

Throughout this paper we rely on the following definitions. The set of natural numbers is given by
{\mathbf{N}}:=\{1,2,3,\ldots\}
, the set
{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}:=\{0,1,2,\ldots\}={\mathbf{N}}\cup\{0\}
, and the set
{\mathbf{Z}}:=\{0,\pm 1,\pm 2,\ldots\}.
The set
{\mathbf{R}}
represents the real numbers and the set
{\mathbf{C}}
represents the complex numbers. For
a_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}},a_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}},a_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}},\ldots\in{\mathbf{C}}
, if
i,j\in{\mathbf{Z}}
and
j<i
then
\sum\limits_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n=i$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n=i$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n=i$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n=i$}}}^{j}a_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}:=0
and
\prod\limits_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n=i$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n=i$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n=i$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n=i$}}}^{j}a_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}:=1
. Note that we often adopt a common notation used for fundamental solution expansions, namely if one takes
a,a^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{R}}
, then

\displaystyle a_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lessgtr$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lessgtr$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lessgtr$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lessgtr$}}}:={\min\atop\max}\{a,a^{\prime}\}.

2.1
The gamma function and factorials

The (Euler) gamma function
\Gamma\colon{\mathbf{C}}\setminus-{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
(see
[
27
, Chapter 5]
), which is ubiquitous in special function theory satisfies the recurrence formula
\Gamma(z+1)=z\Gamma(z)
, and is an important combinatoric function which generalizes the factorial function
\Gamma(n+1)=n!
,
n\in{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
. The gamma function is naturally defined through Euler’s integral
[
27
, equation (5.2.1)]
. The following asymptotic approximation involving the ratio of gamma
functions will also be needed.

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
a,b\in{\mathbf{C}}
. Then we have, as
{0<\tau\rightarrow\infty}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(a\pm{\rm i}\tau)}{\Gamma(b\pm{\rm i}\tau)}={\rm e}^{\pm{\rm i}\pi(a-b)/2}\tau^{a-b}\left\{1+{\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{{1}}{{\tau}}\right)}\right\},

(2.1)

where
\tau^{a-b}
takes its principal value.

Proof.

Let
\delta\in(0,\pi)
. From
[
27
, equation (5.11.13)]

\displaystyle\Gamma(z+a)/\Gamma(z+b)=z^{a-b}\big\{1+{\mathcal{O}\big(z^{-1}\big)}\big\},

(2.2)

as
z\rightarrow\infty
with
a
and
b
real or complex constants, provided
|\arg z|\leq\pi-\delta
(
<\pi
). If one takes
z=\pm{\rm i}\tau
with
\tau>0
then the argument restriction implies
\arg(\pm{\rm i}\tau)=\pm\pi/2
, and (
2.1
) follows.
∎

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
a,b\in{\mathbf{C}}
,
z-a\not\in{\mathbf{Z}}
,
\delta\in(0,\pi)
. Then we have, as
{z\rightarrow\infty}
, provided

|\arg z|\leq\pi-\delta
,

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(-z+a)}{\Gamma(-z+b)}=\frac{\sin(\pi(z-b))}{\sin(\pi(z-a))}z^{a-b}\left\{1+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{z}\right)\right\},

(2.3)

where
z^{a-b}
takes its principal value.

Proof.

Using (
2.2
),
[
27
, equation (5.5.3)]
, the result follows.
∎

The Pochhammer symbol (rising factorial)
(\cdot)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\colon{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
is defined by

\displaystyle(z)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}:=1,\qquad(z)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}:=(z)(z+1)\cdots(z+n-1),

where
n\in{\mathbf{N}}
. Note that
(z)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=\Gamma(z+{n})/\Gamma(z)
,
\Gamma(z-n)=(-1)^{n}\Gamma(z)/(-z+1)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
, for all
z\in{\mathbf{C}}\setminus-{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
,
n\in{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
.

2.2
The Gauss hypergeometric function

The Gauss hypergeometric function
{}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}F_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}\colon{\mathbf{C}}^{2}\times({\mathbf{C}}\setminus-{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}})\times\mathbf{C}\setminus[1,\infty)\to\mathbf{C}
can be defined in terms of the following infinite series
[
27
, equation (15.2.1)]

\displaystyle\,\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}\!\left(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a,b}{c};z\right):=\sum_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n=0$}}}^{\infty}\frac{(a)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(b)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{(c)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}\frac{z^{n}}{n!},\qquad|z|<1,

and elsewhere by analytic continuation. Certain orthogonal polynomials are special cases of the Gauss hypergeometric function, such as Chebyshev polynomials and Gegenbauer polynomials. The Chebyshev polynomial of the first kind
T_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\colon{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
is defined as
[
22
, Section 5.7.2]

\displaystyle T_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(x):=\,\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}\!\left(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{-n,n}{{\tfrac{1}{2}}};\frac{1-x}{2}\right).

Note that
T_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\cos\psi)=\cos(n\psi)
. The Gegenbauer polynomial
C_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{\mu}\colon{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
,
n\in{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
, can be defined in terms of the Gauss hypergeometric function as

\displaystyle C_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{\mu}(x):=\frac{(2\mu)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{n!}\,\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}\!\left(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{-n,2\mu+n}{\mu+{\tfrac{1}{2}}};\frac{1-x}{2}\right).

Note that for
\mu\in(-{\tfrac{1}{2}},\infty)\setminus\{0\}
, the Gegenbauer polynomial is orthogonal on
(-1,1)
with a positive weight (see
[
17
, equation (9.8.20)]
) and identically zero when
\mu=0
for all
n\geq 1
. One also has the following
\mu\to 0
limit holding
[
1
, equation (6.4.13)]

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu\to 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu\to 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu\to 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu\to 0$}}}\frac{{n}+\mu}{\mu}C_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{\mu}(x)=\epsilon_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}T_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(x),

(2.4)

where
\epsilon_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}:=2-\delta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n,0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n,0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n,0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n,0$}}}
. The Legendre polynomial
P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\colon{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
is defined by

\displaystyle P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(x)=P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{0}(x)={\mathsf{P}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(x)={\mathsf{P}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{0}(x)=C_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}(x)

(2.5)

(see Section
2.4
below).

2.3
The associated Legendre functions of the first and second kind

We also frequently use associated Legendre functions of the first and second kind
P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu},Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}\colon{\mathbf{C}}\setminus(-\infty,1]\rightarrow{\mathbf{C}}
respectively. Gauss hypergeometric
representations of these functions are
[
27
, equation (14.3.6)]

\displaystyle P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(z):=\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\mu)}\left(\frac{z+1}{z-1}\right)^{\frac{\mu}{2}}\,\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}\!\left(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{-\nu,\nu+1}{1-\mu};\frac{1-z}{2}\right),

where
|1-z|<2
and
[
27
, equation (14.3.7)]

\displaystyle Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(z):=\frac{\sqrt{\pi}{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\pi\mu}\Gamma(\nu+\mu+1)\big(z^{2}-1\big)^{\frac{\mu}{2}}}{2^{\nu+1}\Gamma\big(\nu+\tfrac{3}{2}\big)z^{\nu+\mu+1}}\,\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}\!\left(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\frac{\nu+\mu+1}{2},\frac{\nu+\mu+2}{2}}{\nu+\tfrac{3}{2}};\frac{1}{z^{2}}\right),

where
|z|>1
. In regard to the above definition of the associated Legendre function of the first kind, note that for
\mu\in{\mathbf{N}}
,
\Gamma(1-\mu)
is undefined, however the function
\tfrac{1}{\Gamma(c)}\,\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}\!\left(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a,b}{c};z\right)
is an entire function for all
a,b,c\in{\mathbf{C}}
,
|z|<1
. See
[
27
, equation (15.2.2)]
and the discussion in
[
27
, Section 14.3(ii)]
for
\mu\in{\mathbf{Z}}
.

The
associated Legendre conical
functions are given by
\mu\in{\mathbf{C}}
,
\tau\geq 0
,
z\in{\mathbf{C}}\setminus(-\infty,1]
,
P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\pm\mu}(z)
,
Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\pm\mu}(z)
. Associated Legendre functions satisfy various transformations such as the Whipple transformation, namely
[
27
, equations (14.9.16) and (14.9.17)]
. They also satisfy various connection relations for (conical) associated Legendre functions of the first kind such as
[
27
, equation (14.9.11)]

\displaystyle P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}-{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}-{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}-{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}-{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\pm\mu}(z)=P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\pm\mu}(z)

(2.6)

for all
\mu
,
\tau
,
z
; and
[
27
, equations (14.3.10) and (14.9.15)]

\displaystyle P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\mu}(z)=\frac{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+{\rm i}\tau\big)}{\Gamma\big({{\frac{1}{2}}}-\mu+{\rm i}\tau\big)}P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{-\mu}(z)+\frac{2}{\pi}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\pi\mu}\sin\left(\mu\pi\right)Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\mu}(z);

(2.7)

connection relations for associated Legendre functions of the second kind, such as
[
27
, equations (14.3.10) and (14.9.14)]

\displaystyle Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{-\mu}(z)={\rm e}^{-2{\rm i}\pi\mu}\frac{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+{\rm i}\tau\big)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+{\rm i}\tau\big)}Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\mu}(z).

(2.8)

For the associated Legendre conical function of the first kind, one has the following important behavior (cf.
[
26
, pp. 171, 173]
)

\displaystyle P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{-\mu}(z)\sim\frac{1}{\Gamma({\mu+1})}\left({\frac{z-1}{2}}\right)^{\frac{\mu}{2}},

(2.9)

as
z\rightarrow 1^{+}
. For the associated Legendre function of the second kind and its conical form we note the following important behaviors

\displaystyle Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(z)\sim\frac{\sqrt{\pi}\Gamma(\nu+\mu+1){\rm e}^{{\rm i}\pi\mu}}{\Gamma\big(\nu+\frac{3}{2}\big)(2z)^{\nu+1}},

(2.10)

as
z\to\infty
,
\nu+\mu+1\neq-1,-2,-3,\ldots
, and

\displaystyle Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\mu}(z)\sim\frac{\sqrt{\pi}{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\pi\mu}\Gamma\left({\mu+{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau}\right)}{\Gamma\left({1\pm{\rm i}\tau}\right)(2z)^{{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau}},

(2.11)

as
z\rightarrow\infty
.

2.3.1
Large degree asymptotics of associated Legendre functions

The associated Legendre functions of the first and second kind satisfy the following asymptotic representations. Let
\mu\geq 0
be bounded,
r\in(0,\infty)
. Then uniformly we have, as
0<\nu\rightarrow\infty

[
27
, equations (14.15.13) and (14.15.14)]
,

\displaystyle P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(\cosh r)=\frac{1}{\nu^{\mu}}\sqrt{\frac{r}{\sinh r}}I_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\big(\big(\nu+{\tfrac{1}{2}}\big)r\big)\left\{1+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\nu}\right)\right\},

(2.12)

\displaystyle Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(\cosh r)={\rm e}^{{\rm i}\pi\mu}\nu^{\mu}\sqrt{\frac{r}{\sinh r}}K_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\big(\big(\nu+{\tfrac{1}{2}}\big)r\big)\left\{1+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\nu}\right)\right\}.

(2.13)

Corresponding results for
P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(\cosh r)
,
Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(\cosh r)
,
Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-\nu-1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-\nu-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-\nu-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-\nu-1$}}}^{\pm\mu}(\cosh r)
follow readily from these formulas and the connection formulas in
[
27
, Section 14.9(iii)]
. For extensions to asymptotic expansions and complex argument see
[
26
, Chapter 12, Section 13]
.

We shall give analogous results for associated Legendre conical functions. In order to do so, we shall require so-called envelope functions for our approximants, the Bessel and Hankel functions. The reason for this is they have zeros in our domain of validity, unlike the modified Bessel functions used in (
2.12
), (
2.13
). For the Bessel function
J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(x)
(
\mu\geq 0
) of real argument
x
, an envelope function
\operatorname{env}J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(x)
is given in
[
27
, equations (2.8.32)–(2.8.34)]
. This function is continuous and nonvanishing, and has the properties that
J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(x)/\operatorname{env}J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(x)={\mathcal{O}}(1)

uniformly for
x\in(0,\infty)
;
\lim\limits_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle x\rightarrow 0+$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle x\rightarrow 0+$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle x\rightarrow 0+$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle x\rightarrow 0+$}}}J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(x)/\operatorname{env}J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(x)>0
; and
J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(x)/\operatorname{env}J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(x)
does not approach zero as
x\rightarrow\infty
. Thus
\operatorname{env}J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(x)
has the same order of magnitude as
J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(x)
uniformly for
x\in(0,\infty)
, but does not have any zeros (except at
x=0
when
\mu>0
).

We would like to define an envelope function for
J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z)
where
z
is complex with
|\arg z|\leq\frac{1}{2}\pi
(again assuming
\mu\geq 0
), and having similar properties as described above. The desired function is given as follows.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
\mu\geq 0
. The function defined by

\displaystyle\operatorname{env}J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z):=\big\{|J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z)|^{2}+|J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu+1$}}}(z)|^{2}\big\}^{\frac{1}{2}},

(2.14)

has the following properties in the half-plane
|\arg z|\leq\frac{1}{2}\pi
:

(i)

it has no zeros, except at
z=0
when
\mu>0
;

(ii)

J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z)/\operatorname{env}J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z)={\mathcal{O}}(1)
uniformly for all non-zero
z
;

(iii)

\lim\limits_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle z\rightarrow 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle z\rightarrow 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle z\rightarrow 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle z\rightarrow 0$}}}|J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z)|/\operatorname{env}J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z)>0
; and

(iv)

J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z)/\operatorname{env}J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z)
does not approach zero as
z\rightarrow\infty
.

Proof.

From
[
27
, equation (10.6.2)]
,
J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{\prime}(z)=(\mu/z)J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z)-J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu+1$}}}(z)
, and since
J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z)
,
J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{\prime}(z)
do not have common zeros (except at
z=0
when
\mu>0
), the same is true of
J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z)
,
J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu+1$}}}(z)
. Thus from (
2.14
) we see (i) holds. Finally (ii)–(iv) follow from (
2.14
) and the following limiting behaviors which hold for
\mu\geq 0
,
|\arg z|\leq\frac{1}{2}\pi
, namely as
z\rightarrow 0
,

\displaystyle J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z)\sim\frac{\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z\big)^{\mu}}{\Gamma(\mu+1)},

(2.15)

and as
z\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z)=\left(\frac{2}{\pi z}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left[\cos\left(z-\frac{\pi\mu}{2}-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)+o(1)\exp\{|{\Im}\,z|\}\right],

which completes the proof.
∎

We also require envelope functions for the Hankel functions
H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1)}(z)
,
H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(2)}(z)
. For positive argument these are not required, since these functions have no real zeros. However they do have complex zeros (see
[
27
, Section 10.21(ix)]
), and for this reason we shall construct them for complex
z
such that
|\arg z|\leq\frac{1}{2}\pi
. To this end we note the limiting behaviors which hold for
|\arg z|\leq\frac{1}{2}\pi
, namely as
z\rightarrow 0
,

\displaystyle H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{(1)}(z)\sim-H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{(2)}(z)\sim\frac{2{\rm i}}{\pi}\log z,

\displaystyle H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1)}(z)\sim-H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(2)}(z)\sim-\frac{{\rm i}\Gamma(\mu)}{\pi}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z\big)^{-\mu},\qquad\mu>0,

(2.16)

and as
z\to\infty
,

\displaystyle H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1,2)}(z)\sim\left(\frac{2}{\pi z}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left\{\pm{\rm i}\left(z-\frac{1}{2}\pi\mu-\frac{1}{4}\pi\right)\right\}.

(2.17)

Now for
H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1)}(z)
the natural choice analogous to (
2.14
) would appear to be
\operatorname{env}H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1)}(z)=\big\{\bigl|H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1)}(z)\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu+1$}}}^{(1)}(z)\bigr|^{2}\big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}
, but on referring to (
2.16
) we see that this function would have the incompatible behavior
\operatorname{env}H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1)}(z)\sim\big|H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu+1$}}}^{(1)}(z)\big|\sim\tfrac{1}{\pi}\Gamma(\mu+1)\big(\tfrac{1}{2}|z|\big)^{-\mu-1}
as
z\rightarrow 0
for
\mu\geq 0
. Thus we would have the undesirable limit
\big|H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1)}(z)\big|/\operatorname{env}H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1)}(z)\rightarrow 0
as
z\rightarrow 0
. A similar problem occurs for such an envelope function for
H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(2)}(z)
. With this in mind, in the following lemma we make the required modification in our definition of the Hankel envelope functions. Note that we omit the proof, which is similar to the proof of Lemma
2.3
.

Lemma 2.4
.

Let
\mu\geq 0
. The functions defined by

\displaystyle\operatorname{env}H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1,2)}(z):=\left\{\bigl|H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1,2)}(z)\bigr|^{2}+\min\bigl\{1,|z|^{2}\bigr\}\bigl|H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu+1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu+1$}}}^{\left(1,2\right)}(z)\bigr|^{2}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}},

(2.18)

has the following properties in the half-plane
|\arg z|\leq\frac{1}{2}\pi
:

(i)

\operatorname{env}H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1,2)}(z)
has no zeros;

(ii)

H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1,2)}(z)/\operatorname{env}H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1,2)}(z)={\mathcal{O}}(1)
uniformly for all non-zero
z
;

(iii)

\lim\limits_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle z\rightarrow 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle z\rightarrow 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle z\rightarrow 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle z\rightarrow 0$}}}\bigl|H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1,2)}(z)\bigr|/\operatorname{env}H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1,2)}(z)>0
; and

(iv)

H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1,2)}(z)/\operatorname{env}H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1,2)}(z)
does not approach zero as
z\rightarrow\infty
.

We now state large degree asymptotic approximations for the associated Legendre conical functions.

Theorem 2.5
.

Let
\mu\geq 0
,
z=\cosh r\in{\mathbf{C}}
, such that
|\arg z|\leq\pi/2

(
equivalently
r
lying in the semi-infinite strip
{\Re}\,r\geq 0
,
|{\Im}\,r|\leq\pi/2
;
see
[
26
, Chapter 12, Section 13.1]
)
. Then uniformly we have, as
0<\tau\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{-\mu}(\cosh r)=\frac{1}{\tau^{\mu}}\sqrt{\frac{r}{\sinh r}}\left\{J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(\tau r)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{{1}}{{\tau}}\right)\operatorname{env}J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(\tau r)\right\},

(2.19)

\displaystyle P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\mu}(\cosh r)=\tau^{\mu}\sqrt{\frac{r}{\sinh r}}\left[\cos(\pi\mu)\left\{J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(\tau r){+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{{1}}{{\tau}}\right)}\operatorname{env}J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(\tau r)\right\}\right.

(2.20)

\displaystyle\left.\hphantom{P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\mu}(\cosh r)=}{}-\sin(\pi\mu)\left\{Y_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(\tau r)+{\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{{1}}{{\tau}}\right)}\operatorname{env}{H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{\left(1\right)}(\tau r)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{{1}}{{\tau}}\right)}\operatorname{env}{H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(2)}(\tau r)}\right\}\right],

\displaystyle Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\pm\mu}(\cosh r)=-\frac{{\rm i}\pi}{2}{\rm e}^{(-1\pm 3){\rm i}\pi\mu/2}\tau^{\pm\mu}\sqrt{\frac{r}{\sinh r}}\left\{{H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(2)}(\tau r)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{{1}}{{\tau}}\right)}\operatorname{env}{H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(2)}(\tau r)}\right\},\!\!\!

(2.21)

\displaystyle Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}-{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}-{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}-{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}-{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\pm\mu}(\cosh r)=\frac{{\rm i}\pi}{2}{\rm e}^{(1\pm 1){\rm i}\pi\mu/2}\tau^{\pm\mu}\sqrt{\frac{r}{\sinh r}}\left\{{H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1)}(\tau r)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{{1}}{{\tau}}\right)}\operatorname{env}{H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1)}(\tau r)}\right\},

(2.22)

where in (
2.20
)–(
2.22
),
r\neq 0
.

Proof.

The functions
w_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1,2$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 1,2$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 1,2$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,2$}}}\colon{\mathbf{C}}\setminus(-\infty,1]\to{\mathbf{C}}
defined by

\displaystyle w_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}(z):=\big({z^{2}-1}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\mu}(z),\qquad w_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}(z):=\big({z^{2}-1}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\mu}(z),

satisfy the following ordinary differential equation

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}^{2}w}{{\rm d}z^{2}}=\left\{{-\frac{\tau^{2}}{z^{2}-1}+\frac{\mu^{2}-1}{\left({z^{2}-1}\right)^{2}}-\frac{1}{4\big(z^{2}-1\big)}}\right\}w.

The first term dominates as
\tau\rightarrow\infty
, and is characterized by having a simple pole at
z=1
. Following
[
26
, Chapter 12]
we simplify it by introducing a new independent variable
\zeta(z)
defined by (see also
[
27
, equation (4.37.19)]
)

\displaystyle\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}:=\int_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}^{z}{\big(t^{2}-1\big)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}t}=\cosh^{-1}z.

(2.23)

Then with the accompanying change of dependent variable
w=\zeta^{-\frac{1}{4}}\big(z^{2}-1\big)^{\frac{1}{4}}W
, we arrive at the transformed differential equation

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}^{2}W}{{\rm d}\zeta^{2}}=\left({-\frac{\tau^{2}}{4\zeta}+\frac{\mu^{2}-1}{4\zeta^{2}}+\frac{\psi(\zeta)}{\zeta}}\right)W,

where

\displaystyle\psi(\zeta):=\frac{4\mu^{2}-1}{16}\left({\frac{1}{z^{2}-1}-\frac{1}{\zeta}}\right),

which is analytic at
z=1
(
\zeta=0)
.

We apply Olver’s theorem
[
26
, Chapter 12, Theorem 9.1]
, with
u=\tau
in the solutions of that theorem (with a slight modification to go from Bessel to modified Bessel functions as described below), and we obtain asymptotic solutions

\displaystyle W_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}(\tau,\zeta)=\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\{J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\big(\tau\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\big){+\mathcal{O}\big(\tau^{-1}\big)}\operatorname{env}J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}{\big(\tau\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\},

(2.24)

\displaystyle W^{(1,2)}(\tau,\zeta)=\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\{{H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1,2)}\big(\tau\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)+\mathcal{O}\big(\tau^{-1}\big)}\operatorname{env}{H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1,2)}\big(\tau\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)}\right\}.

(2.25)

We remark that in Olver’s theorem the approximants used are the modified Bessel functions
I_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}
,
K_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}
. In obtaining the Bessel formulas (
2.24
), (
2.25
) we have used the well-known identities

I_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}({\rm i}z)\propto J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(z)
,
K_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}(\mp{\rm i}{z})\propto H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(1,2)}(z)
.

On account of the monotonicity requirements that determine the domains of validity of the
{\mathcal{O}}
terms in (
2.24
), (
2.25
)
[
26
, Chapter 12, Section 9.1]
, it is straightforward to show that these order terms are uniformly valid in an unbounded
{\zeta}
domain which corresponds to the right half-plane
|\arg z|\leq\frac{1}{2}\pi
; see
[
26
, Chapter 12, Section 13.1]
.

Next we match the associated Legendre conical functions with the asymptotic solutions. To do so we note the fundamental asymptotic behaviors using (
2.9
), (
2.11
). From the behavior of Bessel functions at
0
and
\infty
(see (
2.15
), (
2.17
)), we have as
\zeta\rightarrow 0

\displaystyle W_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}(\tau,\zeta)\sim\zeta^{\frac{{\mu+1}}{2}}\big({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\tau}\big)^{\mu}/\Gamma(\mu+1),

(2.26)

and as
\zeta\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle W^{(1,2)}(\tau,\zeta)\sim\left({\frac{2}{\pi\tau}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\zeta^{\frac{1}{4}}\exp\big\{{\pm{\rm i}\tau\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\mp{\tfrac{1}{2}}{\rm i}\pi\mu\mp{\tfrac{{1}}{4}\pi{\rm i}}}\big\}.

(2.27)

By matching solutions recessive at
z=1
(
\zeta=0)
we therefore deduce that

\displaystyle P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{-\mu}(z)=\lambda_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}(\mu,\tau)\big(\zeta\big(z^{2}-1\big)\big)^{-\frac{1}{4}}W_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}(\tau,\zeta),

(2.28)

for some constant
\lambda_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}(\mu,\tau)
. From (
2.9
), (
2.26
) we find that
\zeta\sim 2(z-1)
as
z\rightarrow 1
, and therefore from (
2.28
)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}(\mu,\tau)=\lim\limits_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle z\rightarrow 1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle z\rightarrow 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle z\rightarrow 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle z\rightarrow 1$}}}\frac{\big(z^{2}-1\big)^{\frac{1}{4}}P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{-\mu}(z)}{\zeta^{\frac{1}{4}}J_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\bigl(\tau\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigr)}=\frac{1}{\tau^{\mu}}.

(2.29)

Now from (
2.23
),
\zeta=r^{2}
for
z=\cosh r
, and therefore from (
2.24
), (
2.28
), (
2.29
) we arrive at (
2.19
). Noting also (
2.6
), which then completes the proof of (
2.19
), (
2.20
).

Next from (
2.23
) it is readily verified that
\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\sim\log(2z)
as
z\rightarrow\infty
. Hence by matching solutions with the same (unique) behavior at
z=\infty
(
\zeta=\infty)
, we assert that
there exists a constant
\lambda^{(2)}(\mu,\tau)
such that

\displaystyle Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\mu}(z)=\lambda^{(2)}(\mu,\tau)\big(\zeta\big(z^{2}-1\big)\big)^{-\frac{1}{4}}W^{(2)}(\tau,\zeta).

This time the constant is found by the following limit

\displaystyle\lambda^{(2)}(\mu,\tau)=\lim\limits_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle z\rightarrow\infty$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle z\rightarrow\infty$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle z\rightarrow\infty$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle z\rightarrow\infty$}}}\frac{\big(z^{2}-1\big)^{\frac{1}{4}}Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\mu}(z)}{\zeta^{\frac{1}{4}}H_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{(2)}\bigl(\tau\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigr)}.

From (
2.11
), (
2.27
) we arrive at

\displaystyle\lambda^{(2)}(\mu,\tau)=\tfrac{1}{2}\pi{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\pi(\frac{\mu}{2}-\frac{1}{4})}\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\Gamma\big(\mu+{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau\big)}{\Gamma(1+{\rm i}\tau)}.

(2.30)

Then, using (
2.1
) to simplify (
2.30
) we establish (
2.21
) for the upper signs. For the lower signs we simply use (
2.8
) together with (
2.1
), (
2.22
) follows similarly. Finally, (
2.20
) comes from (
2.1
), (
2.7
), (
2.19
), (
2.21
), which completes the proof.
∎

We remark that approximations for associated Legendre conical functions as
0<\tau\rightarrow\infty
with
|\mu|
large are given in
[
8
]
(
\mu
real) and
[
9
]
(
\mu
imaginary), but these
are more complicated than those given by (
2.19
)–(
2.22
), as well as in Theorem
2.17
below.

2.3.2
Associated Legendre function addition theorems

In
[
6
, Proposition 5.1]
, an addition theorem is derived for the associated Legendre function of the second kind
Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}^{\mu}(\cosh\rho)/\sinh^{\mu}\rho
, where

\displaystyle\cosh\rho=\cosh r\cosh r^{\prime}-\sinh r\sinh r^{\prime}\cos\gamma.

(2.31)

We now present generalizations of that addition theorem for

\displaystyle P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(\cosh\rho)/\sinh^{\mu}\rho\qquad\text{and}\qquad Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(\cosh\rho)/\sinh^{\mu}\rho.

Theorem 2.6
.

Let
\mu\in{\mathbf{C}}
such that
{\Re}\,\mu>-{\tfrac{1}{2}}
,
\mu\neq 0
,
\nu\in{\mathbf{C}}\setminus-{\mathbf{N}}
,
r,r^{\prime}\in(0,\infty)
,
r\neq r^{\prime}
,
\gamma\in{\mathbf{R}}
and (
2.31
). Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sinh^{\mu}\rho}P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(\cosh\rho)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\frac{2^{\mu}\Gamma(\mu)}{(\sinh r\sinh r^{\prime})^{\mu}}\sum_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n=0$}}}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}(n+\mu)P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-(\mu+n)}(\cosh r_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}})P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu+n}(\cosh r_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}})C_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{\mu}(\cos\gamma),

(2.32)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sinh^{\mu}\rho}Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(\cosh\rho)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\frac{2^{\mu}\Gamma(\mu)}{(\sinh r\sinh r^{\prime})^{\mu}}\sum_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n=0$}}}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}(n+\mu)P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-(\mu+n)}(\cosh r_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}})Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu+n}(\cosh r_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}})C_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{\mu}(\cos\gamma).

(2.33)

For
\mu=0
, see Corollary
2.7
.

Proof.

The proof for the expansion of the associated Legendre function of the second kind is identical to that given in
[
6
, Proposition 5.1]
, except one does not specialize to
\nu=\mu
. For the associated Legendre function of the first kind, instead start with
[
10
, equation (8.3)]
.
∎

Corollary 2.7
.

Let
\nu\in{\mathbf{C}}\setminus-{\mathbf{N}}
,
r,r^{\prime}\in(0,\infty)
,
r\neq r^{\prime}
,
\gamma\in{\mathbf{R}}
and (
2.31
). Then

\displaystyle P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}(\cosh\rho)=\sum_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n=0$}}}^{\infty}\epsilon_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(-1)^{n}P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-n}(\cosh r_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}})P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{n}(\cosh r_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}})T_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\cos\gamma),

\displaystyle Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}(\cosh\rho)=\sum_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n=0$}}}^{\infty}\epsilon_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(-1)^{n}P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-n}(\cosh r_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}})Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{n}(\cosh r_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}})T_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\cos\gamma).

(2.34)

Proof.

Taking the limit as
\mu\to 0
in (
2.33
) and using (
2.4
) produces the result.
∎

Corollary 2.8
.

Let
\nu\in{\mathbf{C}}\setminus-{\mathbf{N}}
,
r,r^{\prime}\in(0,\infty)
,
r\neq r^{\prime}
,
\gamma\in{\mathbf{R}}
and (
2.31
). Then

\displaystyle\frac{\cosh\big(\big(\nu+\frac{1}{2}\big)\rho\big)}{\sinh\rho}=\frac{\pi}{2\sqrt{\sinh r\sinh r^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\frac{\cosh\big(\big(\nu+\frac{1}{2}\big)\rho\big)}{\sinh\rho}=}{}\times\sum_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n=0$}}}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}(2n+1)P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-n-\frac{1}{2}}(\cosh r_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}})P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}(\cosh r_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}})P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\cos\gamma),

\displaystyle\frac{\exp\big({-}\big(\nu+\frac{1}{2}\big)\rho\big)}{\sinh\rho}=\frac{-{\rm i}}{\sqrt{\sinh r\sinh r^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\frac{\exp\big({-}\big(\nu+\frac{1}{2}\big)\rho\big)}{\sinh\rho}=}{}\times\sum_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n=0$}}}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}(2n+1)P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-n-\frac{1}{2}}(\cosh r_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}})Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}(\cosh r_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}})P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\cos\gamma).

Proof.

Let
\mu=\tfrac{1}{2}
in (
2.32
), (
2.33
), and using
[
27
, equations (14.5.15) and (14.5.17)]
, (
2.5
), completes the proof.
∎

2.4
The Ferrers functions of the first and second kind

The Ferrers functions of the first and second kind respectively
\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu},\mathsf{Q}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}\colon(-1,1)\rightarrow{\mathbf{C}}
provide a definition for the associated Legendre functions on
(-1,1)
. The Ferrers functions have the following Gauss hypergeometric representations
[
27
, equations (14.3.1) and (14.3.12)]

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(x):=\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\mu)}\left(\frac{1+x}{1-x}\right)^{\frac{\mu}{2}}\,\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}\!\left(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{-\nu,\nu+1}{1-\mu};\frac{1-x}{2}\right),

(2.35)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(x):=\sqrt{\pi}\,2^{\mu}\frac{\Gamma\big(\frac{\nu+\mu+2}{2}\big)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{\nu-\mu+1}{2}\big)}\frac{x\cos\big[\frac{\pi}{2}(\nu+\mu)\big]}{\big(1-x^{2}\big)^{\frac{\mu}{2}}}\,\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}\!\left(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\frac{1-\nu-\mu}{2},\frac{\nu-\mu+2}{2}}{\tfrac{3}{2}};x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hphantom{{\mathsf{Q}}_{\nu}^{\mu}(x):=}{}-\sqrt{\pi}2^{\mu-1}\frac{\Gamma\big(\frac{\nu+\mu+1}{2}\big)}{\Gamma\big(\frac{\nu-\mu+2}{2}\big)}\frac{\sin\big[\frac{\pi}{2}(\nu+\mu)\big]}{\big(1-x^{2}\big)^{\frac{\mu}{2}}}\,\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}\!\left(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\frac{-\nu-\mu}{2},\frac{\nu-\mu+1}{2}}{{\tfrac{1}{2}}};x^{2}\right),

(2.36)

where for
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}
we must impose the constraint
\nu+\mu\in{\mathbf{C}}\setminus-{\mathbf{N}}
, except for the anomalous cases
\nu=-\frac{3}{2},-\frac{5}{2},\ldots
. In regard to the above definition of the Ferrers function of the first kind (see also the definition of
P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(z)
given in Section
2.3
), note that
\tfrac{1}{\Gamma(c)}\,\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}\!\left(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a,b}{c};z\right)
is an entire function for all
a,b,c\in{\mathbf{C}}
,
|z|<1
. See
[
27
, equation (15.2.2)]
and the discussion in
[
27
, Section 14.3(ii)]
for
\mu\in{\mathbf{Z}}
.

Note that the Ferrers function of the first kind is related to the Gegenbauer function of the first kind (generalization of the Gegenbauer polynomial for non-integer degrees) with general
\lambda
,
\mu
, using
[
27
, equation (14.3.21)]
, namely

\displaystyle C_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda$}}}^{\mu}(\cos\gamma)=\frac{\sqrt{\pi}\,\Gamma(2\mu+\lambda)}{2^{\mu-\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma(\lambda+1)\,\Gamma(\mu)}\left({\sin\gamma}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu+\lambda-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu+\lambda-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu+\lambda-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu+\lambda-\frac{1}{2}$}}}^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}(\cos\gamma).

(2.37)

The
Ferrers conical
functions are given by
\mu\in{\mathbf{C}}
,
\tau\geq 0
,
x\in(-1,1)
,
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\pm\mu}(x)
,
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}\pm{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\pm\mu}(x)
, which satisfy the following connection formulas, namely
[
27
, equation (14.9.11)]

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}-{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}-{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}-{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}-{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\pm\mu}(x)=\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-{\frac{1}{2}}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{\pm\mu}(x)

(2.38)

and from
[
27
, equation (14.9.10)]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-\frac{1}{2}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{-\mu}(-x)=\sin(\pi{({\rm i}\tau-\mu)})\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-\frac{1}{2}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{-\mu}(x)+\frac{2}{\pi}\cos(\pi({\rm i}\tau-\mu))\mathsf{Q}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-\frac{1}{2}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}+{\rm i}\tau$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}+{\rm i}\tau$}}}^{-\mu}(x).

(2.39)

For the Ferrers functions of the first and second kind, one has the connection formulas, namely
[
27
, Section 14.9(ii)]
, and after replacing
\mu
by
-\mu
one obtains

\displaystyle{\sf P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(x)=\frac{\Gamma(\nu-\mu+1)}{\Gamma(\nu+\mu+1)}\left[\cos(\pi\mu){\sf P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(x)-\frac{2}{\pi}\sin(\pi\mu){\sf Q}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(x)\right],

(2.40)

\displaystyle{\sf Q}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(x)=\frac{\Gamma(\nu-\mu+1)}{\Gamma(\nu+\mu+1)}\left[\cos(\pi\mu){\sf Q}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(x)+\frac{\pi}{2}\sin(\pi\mu){\sf P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(x)\right].

(2.41)

We will also need
[
27
, equations (14.9.8) and (14.9.10)]
,

\displaystyle{\sf P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(-x)=\cos(\pi(\nu-\mu)){\sf P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(x)-\frac{2}{\pi}\sin(\pi(\nu-\mu)){\sf Q}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(x),

(2.42)

\displaystyle{\sf Q}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(-x)=-\cos(\pi(\nu-\mu)){\sf Q}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(x)-\frac{\pi}{2}\sin(\pi(\nu-\mu)){\sf P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(x).

(2.43)

2.4.1
Some properties of functions associated with
\boldsymbol{{\sf P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(-x)}

Proposition 2.9
.

Let
x\in(-1,1)
,
\nu\in{\mathbf{C}}
,
\Re\mu>0
. Then

\displaystyle{\sf P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(-x)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\mu+1)}\left(\frac{1+x}{1-x}\right)^{\frac{\mu}{2}}\,\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}\!\left(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{-\nu,\nu+1}{1+\mu};\frac{1+x}{2}\right).

(2.44)

Proof.

Use (
2.35
) and map
x\mapsto-x
,
\mu\mapsto-\mu
.
∎

Corollary 2.10
.

Let
x\in(-1,1)
,
\nu\in{\mathbf{C}}
,
\Re\mu>0
. Then

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle x\to-1^{+}$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle x\to-1^{+}$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle x\to-1^{+}$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle x\to-1^{+}$}}}{\sf P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(-x)=0,

(2.45)

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle x\to-1^{+}$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle x\to-1^{+}$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle x\to-1^{+}$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle x\to-1^{+}$}}}\frac{1}{\big(1-x^{2}\big)^{\frac{\mu}{2}}}{\sf P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(-x)=\frac{1}{2^{\mu}\Gamma(\mu+1)}.

(2.46)

Proof.

As
x\to-1^{+}
(
2.44
) clearly vanishes, establishing (
2.45
). Dividing (
2.44
) by
\big(1-x^{2}\big)^{\frac{\mu}{2}}
and estimating as
x\to-1^{+}
establishes (
2.46
).
∎

Corollary 2.11
.

Let
x\in(-1,1)
,
\nu\in{\mathbf{C}}
. Then
