If
\beta=1/(2R)
then the fundamental solution
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle R,1/(2R)$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle R,1/(2R)$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle R,1/(2R)$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R,1/(2R)$}}}^{2,-}
reduces to a complete elliptic integral of the first kind through
[
27
, equations (14.5.24)–(14.5.27)]
, namely

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle R,1/(2R)$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle R,1/(2R)$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle R,1/(2R)$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R,1/(2R)$}}}^{2,-}({\bf x},{{\bf x}^{\prime}})=\frac{1}{2\pi}\hskip 2.22223pt\mathrm{sech}\hskip 1.66667pt\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\rho\big)K\big(\hskip 2.22223pt\mathrm{sech}\hskip 1.66667pt\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\rho\big)\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{{\mathcal{H}}_{R,1/(2R)}^{2,-}({\bf x},{{\bf x}^\prime})}{}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle m=0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle m=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle m=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle m=0$}}}^{\infty}\epsilon_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle m$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle m$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle m$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle m$}}}(-1)^{m}P_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}$}}}^{-m}(\cosh r_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}})Q_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-\frac{1}{2}$}}}^{m}(\cosh r_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}})\cos(m(\phi-\phi^{\prime})).

Similar expansions are possible for the other fundamental solutions.

Two-dimensions is the only case where we are able to compute azimuthal Fourier expansions of our fundamental solutions whose coefficients are given without a sum. For dimensions greater than two, the approach used previously for Laplace’s equation
[
6
, Section 4]
does not easily generalize for the Helmholtz operator.

Appendix A
Proof of the addition Theorem
2.19

Since the proof of the addition Theorem
2.19
is fairly long, we have delegated it to this appendix. We begin by studying the convergence of (
2.74
)–(
2.77
) by determining the asymptotic behavior of the
n
th terms as
n\rightarrow\infty
. To this end, for (
2.76
) use
[
27
, equation (14.15.1)]
, and from this we deduce that as
{\Re}\mu\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(\cos\theta)\sim\frac{\tan^{\mu}\bigl(\frac{1}{2}\theta\bigr)}{\Gamma(1+\mu)},\qquad\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-\mu}(-\cos\theta)\sim\frac{\cot^{\mu}\big({{\frac{1}{2}}\theta}\big)}{\Gamma(1+\mu)}.

(A.1)

Then, from the large argument asymptotics for the gamma function (Stirling’s formula
[
27
, equation (5.11.3)]
) we obtain

\displaystyle(n+\mu)(\nu+\mu+1)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\mu-\nu)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-(\mu+n)}\bigl(\cos\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\bigr)\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-(\mu+n)}\bigl({\cos\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad{}\sim\frac{\big\{{\tan\bigl({{\frac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}}\bigr)\tan\bigl({{\frac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}}\bigr)}\big\}^{\mu+n}}{\Gamma(\nu+\mu+1)\Gamma(\mu-\nu)}.

(A.2)

Using the connection formula
[
27
, equation (14.9.7)]
and (
A.1
) we have as
{\Re}\mu\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(\cos\theta)\sim\frac{\Gamma(\nu+\mu+1)\Gamma(\mu-\nu)}{\pi\Gamma(\mu+1)}\big\{{-}\sin(\nu\pi)\tan^{\mu}\big({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\theta}\big)+\sin(\mu\pi)\cot^{\mu}\bigl({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\theta}\bigr)\big\}.

(A.3)

Hence, for (
2.74
), from (
A.1
), (
A.3
), and Stirling’s formula, one obtains as
n\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle(n+\mu)\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-(\mu+n)}\bigl(\cos\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\bigr)\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu+n}(\cos\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}})

\displaystyle\qquad{}\sim\frac{1}{\pi}\tan^{\mu+n}\bigl({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}}\bigr)\big\{{-}\sin({\nu\pi})\tan^{\mu+n}\bigl({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}}\bigr)+({-1})^{n}\sin\left({\mu\pi}\right)\cot^{\mu+n}\bigl({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}}\bigr)\big\}.

(A.4)

For (
2.75
), using the connection formula
[
27
, equation (14.9.9)]
, one has as
n\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle(n+\mu)\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-(\mu+n)}(\cos\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}})\mathsf{Q}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu+n}(\cos\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}})

\displaystyle\qquad{}\sim\frac{1}{2}\tan^{\mu+n}\bigl({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}}\bigr)\big\{{-}\cos(\nu\pi)\tan^{\mu+n}\bigl({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}}\bigr)+({-1})^{n}\cos(\mu\pi)\cot^{\mu+n}\bigl({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}}\bigr)\big\}.

(A.5)

Similarly for (
2.77
), using (
A.1
), the connection formula
[
27
, equation (14.9.10)]
, and Stirling’s formula, yields for
\mu-\nu\notin{\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle(n+\mu)(\nu+\mu+1)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\mu-\nu)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-({\mu+n})}\bigl({\cos\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}}\bigr)\mathsf{Q}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{-({\mu+n})}(\cos\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}})\sim\frac{\pi\tan^{\mu+n}\bigl({{\frac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}}\bigr)}{2\Gamma(\nu+\mu+1)\Gamma(\mu-\nu)}

\displaystyle\qquad{}\times\big\{{\cot({(\nu-\mu)\pi})\tan^{\mu+n}\bigl({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}}\bigr)+(-1)^{n+1}\csc((\nu-\mu)\pi)\cot^{\mu+n}\bigl({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}}\bigr)}\big\}.

(A.6)

It remains to estimate the Gegenbauer polynomials of the first kind which appear in (
2.74
)–(
2.76
). We were not able to find asymptotic results in the literature for large
n
and complex parameter
\mu
. However the following estimate will suffice.

Proposition A.1
.

For
\gamma\in(0,\pi)
and
\mu\in{\mathbf{C}}
bounded, one has as
n\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle C_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{\mu}(\cos\gamma)=\mathcal{O}\big({n^{{\Re}\mu-1}}\big).

(A.7)

Proof.

Assume first that
{\Re}\mu>0.
Then for
\gamma\in(0,\pi)
we use the following integral representation
[
27
, equation (14.12.1)]

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu+n-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu+n-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu+n-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu+n-\frac{1}{2}$}}}^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}(\cos\gamma)=\frac{\sqrt{2}({\sin\gamma})^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}}{\sqrt{\pi}\Gamma(\mu)}\int_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{\gamma}\frac{\cos({(\mu+n)t})}{({\cos t-\cos\gamma})^{1-\mu}}\mathrm{d}t.

(A.8)

Then with
\cos\theta={\frac{1}{2}}\big({{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\theta}+{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\theta}}\big)
, and substituting
t\mapsto\gamma-t
, one arrives at

\displaystyle\int_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{\gamma}\frac{\cos({(\mu+n)t})}{({\cos t-\cos\gamma})^{1-\mu}}\mathrm{d}t

(A.9)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\frac{{\rm e}^{{\rm i}(\mu+n)\gamma}}{2}\int_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{\gamma}\frac{{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\mu t}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}nt}}{({\cos({\gamma-t})-\cos\gamma})^{1-\mu}}\mathrm{d}t+\frac{{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}(\mu+n)\gamma}}{2}\int_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{\gamma}\frac{{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\mu t}{\rm e}^{{\rm i}nt}}{({\cos({\gamma-t})-\cos\gamma})^{1-\mu}}\mathrm{d}t.

Thus from (
A.8
), (
A.9
), one has for
\gamma\in(0,\pi)
,
{\Re}\mu>0
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu+n-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu+n-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu+n-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu+n-\frac{1}{2}$}}}^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}(\cos\gamma)=\frac{\sqrt{2}({\sin\gamma})^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}}{\sqrt{\pi}\Gamma(\mu)}\left[{\int_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{\infty}\phi_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle+$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}(t){\rm e}^{{\rm i}nt}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{\infty}\phi_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}(t){\rm e}^{-{\rm i}nt}\mathrm{d}t}\right],

(A.10)

where
\phi_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}\colon(0,\infty)\rightarrow{\mathbf{C}}
are defined by

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}(t):=\frac{{\rm e}^{\mp{\rm i}(\mu+n)\gamma}{\rm e}^{\pm{\rm i}\mu t}H({\gamma-t})}{2({\cos({\gamma-t})-\cos\gamma})^{1-\mu}},

where
H(x)
is the Heaviside function
[
27
, equation (1.16.13)]
. Next
\phi_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}(t)\!\sim\!{\frac{1}{2}}{\rm e}^{\mp{\rm i}(\mu+n)\gamma}({t\sin\gamma})^{\mu-1}
as
t\rightarrow 0
and therefore from the asymptotics of Fourier integrals
[
37
, Section 4.2, Theorem 1, p. 199]
applied to both integrals on the right-hand side of (
A.10
) we deduce for
\gamma\in(0,\pi)
,
{\Re}\mu>0
,
n\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mu+n-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\mu+n-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mu+n-\frac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu+n-\frac{1}{2}$}}}^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}(\cos\gamma)=\mathcal{O}\big({n^{-{\Re}\mu}}\big).

The estimate (
A.7
) then follows from (
2.37
) with
\lambda=n
and Stirling’s formula. Finally, to extend this to bounded
\mu
lying in the left half plane, we have from
[
27
, equation (18.9.7)]

\displaystyle C_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{\mu-1}(\cos\gamma)=\frac{\mu-1}{n+\mu-1}\big(C_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{\mu}(\cos\gamma)-C_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n-2$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n-2$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n-2$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n-2$}}}^{\mu}(\cos\gamma)\big)=\mathcal{O}\big({n^{{\Re}\mu-2}}\big),

(A.11)

for
{\Re}\mu>0
. Hence (
A.7
) holds for
{\Re}\mu>-1
, and on repeated applications of (
A.11
), for
{\Re}\mu>-m
for arbitrary bounded positive integer
m
.
∎

Theorem A.2
.

The series in (
2.76
) is absolutely convergent under the condition (
2.73
). The series in (
2.74
), (
2.75
), (
2.77
)–(
2.79
) are absolutely convergent if (
2.73
) holds, and in addition
\theta\neq\theta^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Using Proposition
A.1
, we see that absolute convergence of (
2.74
), (
2.75
), (
2.77
) is assured if the right-hand sides of (
A.2
), (
A.4
)–(
A.6
) are exponentially small in
n
. For (
2.76
), we see from (
A.2
) that this is so if (
2.73
) holds. Similarly we see that (
A.4
)–(
A.6
) are exponentially small in
n
provided
both
of the following hold (unless one of the coefficient trigonometric functions vanish, in which case one of these can be relaxed, as described below)

\displaystyle\tan\bigl({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}}\bigr)\tan\bigl({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}}\bigr)<1\qquad\text{and}\qquad\tan\bigl({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}}\bigr)\cot\bigl({{\tfrac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}}\bigr)<1.

(A.12)

But the latter of (
A.12
) is equivalent to
\tan\bigl({{\frac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}}\bigr)<\tan\bigl({{\frac{1}{2}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}}\bigr)
, i.e.,
\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}<\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}
, or equivalently
\theta\neq\theta^{\prime}
. Thus
convergence of (
2.74
), (
2.75
), (
2.77
) is also assured by condition (
2.73
) provided
\theta\neq\theta^{\prime}
. Absolute convergence of the expansions (
2.78
), (
2.79
) is proved similarly.
∎

Remark A.3
.

The condition
\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle<$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle<$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle<$}}}\neq\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle>$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle>$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle>$}}}
or equivalently
\theta\neq\theta^{\prime}
, can be relaxed for (
2.74
) if
\mu
is an integer, and for (
2.75
) if
\mu
is half an odd integer. Interestingly, if
\nu
is an integer, then from (
A.4
), (
A.5
) we see that (
2.74
), (
2.75
) converge without requiring (
2.73
), with the only requirement in this case being
\theta\neq\theta^{\prime}
.

Assume (
2.73
) and define

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}({z;\lambda,\theta,{\theta}^{\prime}}):=\sum\limits_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n=0$}}}^{\infty}{(-1)^{n}({n+\lambda})({2\lambda+z+1})_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}({-z})_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathsf{A}( {z;\lambda,\theta,{\theta}^{\prime}}):=}{}\times\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda+z$}}}^{-({\lambda+n})}({\cos\theta})\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda+z$}}}^{-({\lambda+n})}({\cos{\theta}^{\prime}})C_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{\lambda}(\cos\gamma).

(A.13)

Note that for all
n\in{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
,

\displaystyle({2\lambda+z+1})_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}({-z})_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=\frac{\Gamma({n+2\lambda+z+1})\Gamma({n-z})}{\Gamma({2\lambda+z+1})\Gamma({-z})}=(-1)^{n}\frac{\Gamma({n+2\lambda+z+1})\Gamma({z+1})}{\Gamma({2\lambda+z+1})\Gamma({z+1-n})},

and is analytic for all
z\in{\mathbf{C}}
.

Lemma A.4
.

Let
l\in{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}({l;\lambda,\theta,{\theta}^{\prime}})=\sum\limits_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n=0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n=0$}}}^{l}{(-1)^{n}({n+\lambda})({2\lambda+l+1})_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}({-l})_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda+l$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda+l$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda+l$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda+l$}}}^{-({\lambda+n})}({\cos\theta})\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda+l$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda+l$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda+l$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda+l$}}}^{-({\lambda+n})}({\cos{\theta}^{\prime}})C_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{\lambda}(\cos\gamma)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathsf{A}( {l;\lambda,\theta,{\theta}^{\prime}})}{}=\frac{1}{2^{\lambda}\Gamma(\lambda)}\left(\frac{{\sin\theta\sin{\theta}^{\prime}}}{\sin\Theta}\right)^{\lambda}\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda+l$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda+l$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda+l$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda+l$}}}^{-\lambda}({\cos\Theta}).

(A.14)

Proof.

The first equality of (
A.14
) comes from (
A.13
) and noting that
(-l)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=0
for
n\geq l+1
. Next, use the addition theorem for Gegenbauer polynomials
[
27
, equation (18.18.8)]
with degree
l
, which is a finite sum over
n=0,1,2,\ldots,l
. The Gegenbauer polynomial on the left-hand side and the first two Gegenbauer polynomials appearing in the sum are expressed in terms of Ferrers functions of the first kind using (
2.37
). Performing these replacements and simplifying produces the second equality of (
A.14
).
∎

In what follows we shall make use the following uniqueness theorem of complex analysis, due to Carlson (cf.
[
33
, Section 5.81]
).

Theorem A.5
.

Let
\kappa<\pi
,
\mathsf{F}\colon{\mathbf{C}}\rightarrow{\mathbf{C}}
be analytic and
\mathcal{O}\big({\rm e}^{\kappa|z|}\big)
for
\Re z\geq 0
, and in addition
\mathsf{F}(z)=0
for
z\in{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
. Then
\mathsf{F}(z)=0
.

Remark A.6
.

The order restriction as stated is only required for
\Re z=0
, and can be relaxed to
\mathsf{F}(z)
being of (arbitrary) exponential type for
\Re z>0
; however the above weaker version of Carlson’s
theorem suffices for our purposes.

In order to apply the above theorem, we need estimates on
\mathsf{A}({z;\lambda,\theta,\theta^{\prime}})
for large complex
z
. To do so, we need to temporarily assume that
{\theta}
,
{{\theta}^{\prime}}
are
complex. To this end, we again use
[
27
, equation (14.12.1)]
to obtain the integral representation for
{\theta}\in(0,\pi)
,
{\Re}\mu>-\frac{1}{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda+z$}}}^{-\mu}({\cos\theta})=\left({\frac{2}{\pi}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\Gamma\big({\mu+{\frac{1}{2}}}\big)}\int_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{\theta}{\frac{\cos\big({\big({\lambda+z+{\frac{1}{2}}}\big)t}\big)}{({\cos t-\cos\theta})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left({\frac{\cos t-\cos\theta}{\sin\theta}}\right)^{\mu}\mathrm{d}t}.

(A.15)

Taking principal branches, we then use this to (uniquely) define
\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda+z$}}}^{-\mu}({\cos\theta})
as an analytic function of
\theta\in\mathbf{C}
with
{\Re}\theta\in(0,\pi)
. Then if we choose the path of integration in (
A.15
) to be a straight line from
t=0
to
t=\theta
, parameterize
t=s\theta
(
0\leq s\leq 1
), and use a Maclaurin series for trigonometric functions, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\cos t-\cos\theta}{\sin\theta}=\frac{1}{2}\big(1-s^{2}\big)\theta+{\mathcal{O}}\big(\theta^{3}\big).

(A.16)

In addition, for
t
lying on this path we have

\displaystyle\cos\big({\big({\lambda+z+{\tfrac{1}{2}}}\big)t}\big)=\cos\big({\big({\lambda+z+{\tfrac{1}{2}}}\big)}s\theta\big)={\mathcal{O}}\big({\rm e}^{|{\theta z}|}\big).

(A.17)

From (
A.15
), (
A.16
), (
A.17
) it follows that for complex values of
\theta
such that
{\Re}\theta>0
,
{|\theta|<}\frac{1}{2}\pi-1
(a bound clarified below), there is an assignable positive number
a_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
such that

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda+z$}}}^{-\mu}({\cos\theta})=\frac{({a}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}{|\theta|})^{{\Re}\mu}{\rm e}^{|{\theta z}|}}{\Gamma\big({\mu+{\frac{1}{2}}}\big)}{\mathcal{O}}(1),

(A.18)

uniformly for one or both
|z|
and
{\Re}\mu
large.

Next from
[
27
, equations (4.5.13) and (5.6.1)]
and the definition of the Pochhammer symbol, we have for unbounded positive
n
, and
w\in\mathbf{C}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\bigl|{(w)_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}\bigr|}{\Gamma(n)}\leq\frac{n^{\frac{1}{2}}{\rm e}^{n}|w|({|w|+1})({|w|+2})\cdots({|w|+n})}{({2\pi})^{\frac{1}{2}}n^{n}}\leq\frac{n^{\frac{1}{2}}{\rm e}^{n}}{({2\pi})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left({1+\frac{|w|}{n}}\right)^{n}\leq\frac{n^{\frac{1}{2}}{\rm e}^{n}{\rm e}^{|w|}}{({2\pi})^{\frac{1}{2}}},

and therefore from (
A.18
) and Stirling’s formula,

\displaystyle({n+\lambda})({2\lambda+z+1})_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}({-z})_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda+z$}}}^{-({\lambda+n})}({\cos\theta})\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda+z$}}}^{-({\lambda+n})}({\cos{\theta}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma^{2}(n)}{\Gamma^{2}\big({n+\lambda+{\frac{1}{2}}}\big)}\big({a}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{2}{{\rm e}^{2}|{\theta{\theta}^{\prime}}|}\big)^{n}{\rm e}^{({2+|\theta|+|{{\theta}^{\prime}}|})|z|}{\mathcal{O}}\big({n^{2}}\big)=\big({a}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{2}{{\rm e}^{2}|{\theta{\theta}^{\prime}}|}\big)^{n}{\rm e}^{({2+|\theta|+|{{\theta}^{\prime}}|})|z|}{\mathcal{O}}\big({n^{1-2{\Re}\lambda}}\big).

Using (
A.7
), along with the temporary assumption that
|\theta|,|{{\theta}^{\prime}}|\leq\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}<\frac{1}{2}\pi-1
(for some positive
\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
, to be specified shortly) we arrive at

\displaystyle({n+\lambda})({2\lambda+z+1})_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}({-z})_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda+z$}}}^{-({\lambda+n})}({\cos\theta})\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda+z$}}}^{-({\lambda+n})}({\cos{\theta}^{\prime}})C_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle n$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{\lambda}({\cos\gamma})

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\delta^{n}{\rm e}^{\kappa|z|}{\mathcal{O}}\big({n^{-{\Re}\lambda}}\big),

(A.19)

where
\kappa:=2+|\theta|+|{{\theta}^{\prime}}|\leq 2+2\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}<\pi
, and
\delta:={a}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{2}{\rm e}^{2}|{\theta{\theta}^{\prime}}|\leq{a}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{2}{\rm e}^{2}\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{2}
. We wish that
\delta<1
, and this is achieved by choosing
\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
so that
\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\in\big(0,\min\big\{\frac{1}{2}\pi-1,{a}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{-1}{\rm e}^{-1}\big\}\big)
.

Next, from (
2.72
), we observe that
\Theta\rightarrow 0
as
|\theta|+|{{\theta}^{\prime}}|\rightarrow 0
, and therefore there exists a positive
\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}
such that
|\Theta|\leq\kappa<\pi
when
\max\{|\theta|,|\theta^{\prime}|\}\leq\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}
. Now define
\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}:=\min\{\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}},\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}\}
, and also define

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}({z;\lambda,\theta,{\theta}^{\prime}}):=\frac{1}{2^{\lambda}\Gamma(\lambda)}\left(\frac{{\sin\theta\sin{\theta}^{\prime}}}{\sin\Theta}\right)^{\lambda}\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda+z$}}}^{-\lambda}({\cos\Theta})-\mathsf{A}({z;\lambda,\theta,{\theta}^{\prime}}).

(A.20)

Then from (
A.13
), (
A.18
), (
A.19
), we see that for
|\theta|,|{{\theta}^{\prime}}|\in(0,\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}})
,
{\Re}z\geq 0
, the function
\mathsf{F}({z;\lambda,\theta,{\theta}^{\prime}})
is well-defined and analytic as a function of
z
, and is
{\mathcal{O}}\big({{\rm e}^{\kappa|z|}}\big)
as
z\rightarrow\infty
, where
\kappa<\pi
. Moreover from (
A.14
), (
A.20
), we see that
\mathsf{F}({z;\lambda,\theta,{\theta}^{\prime}})=0
for
z\in{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
, and therefore by Carlson’s Theorem
A.5
we deduce that
\mathsf{F}({z;\lambda,\theta,{\theta}^{\prime}})
is identically zero.

The restriction
0<|\theta|,|{{\theta}^{\prime}}|<\theta_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}
can be relaxed as follows. For fixed
z
,
\lambda
, the function given by (
A.20
) is also analytic in
\theta
in a neighborhood of the origin with
{\Re}\theta>0
, and likewise for
{\theta}^{\prime}
. Hence by analytic continuation, we deduce that it must be identically zero in larger domains (open and connected) containing this neighborhood, and for which
\mathsf{P}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda+z$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda+z$}}}^{-\lambda}({\cos\Theta})
is analytic in both of these variables, and for which the series in (
A.13
) is absolutely convergent. In particular, it follows that
\mathsf{F}({z;\lambda,\theta,{\theta}^{\prime}})=0
for positive values of these variables such that
\tan\bigl({{\frac{1}{2}}\theta}\bigr)\tan\bigl({{\frac{1}{2}}{\theta}^{\prime}}\bigr)<1
. The identity (
2.76
) follows by replacing
z\mapsto\nu-\lambda
,
\lambda\mapsto\mu
, in (
A.20
) and equating this function to zero.

It remains to prove (
2.74
), (
2.75
), (
2.77
)–(
2.79
). These come from (
2.76
) and connection formulas. In (
2.76
), apply the connection relation (
2.40
) to the Ferrers function of the first kind on the left-hand side and the one furthest to the right on the right-hand side. This produces two separate sums which are given by (
2.74
), (
2.75
). In (
2.75
), applying the connection relation
[
27
, equation (14.9.1)]
to the left-hand side, and to the Ferrers function of the second kind furthest on the right, produces yet two more sums, one being (
2.77
). The addition theorems (
2.78
), (
2.79
), then follow using (
2.42
), (
2.43
) in (
2.76
), (
2.77
). This completes the proof of Theorem
2.19
.

Remark A.7
.

In terms of the validity of the series expansions, it should be noted that the Ferrers function of the second kind
\mathsf{Q}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\displaystyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\textstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\nu$}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}}}^{\mu}(x)
is not defined for
\nu+\mu=-1,-2,\ldots
, except for the anomalous cases
\nu=-\frac{3}{2},-\frac{5}{2},\ldots
, see (
2.36
).
